changed earlier, ~L599-L632):

   _bg.get("step_name") is empty (only add the key when it has a value).

3. Ensure publish_message(... "Error" ...) runs regardless of whether the DB 
   write succeeded.
4. Do not change anything else. Show the full diff.

Searched for regex `def publish_message|def start_progress|heartbeat_job_state\(|async def publish_progress|REDIS_PROGRESS_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS|async def stop_progress|create_task|if not channel_name|r.publish|await self.task|for ik|MAX_L2_ATTEMPTS|response = await llm.ainvoke|state\["error"\]|workflow.ainvoke|total_opex_costs=|l1_allocations=agentic|if not total_opex_costs|if total_opex_costs is None` (`**/{redis_cache,allocate_opex,agentic_fit_allocator}.py`), 40 results

**Findings**
The clearest defect is **failed L2 jobs never publish failure**. The suspected `reasoning` mismatch is **not present in the L1-to-L2 reader**. The original exception or stalled operation remains unverified without the affected jobs’ stored inputs and logs.

1. **Highest confidence: allocation exceptions leave the job loading.** The handler checks `"substepkey" in locals()`, but that variable is never assigned. Consequently, failed DB status and Redis `"Error"` publication are both skipped: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:606`, `agentic_fit_allocator.py:617`. An isolated execution of the actual function with mocked dependencies reproduced: DB `2/25%` twice → Started → monitor stopped → email attempted, **no failed write and no Error event**. The outer runner only logs the escaped exception: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:132`.

2. **Likely trigger, conditional on payload: L2 loses total OpEx during the L1 handoff.** L2 reads `capabilityInfo.total_opex` separately from `capabilityInfo.AgenticFitGrid`: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:210`. L1 output contains `AgenticFitGrid`, whose state includes numeric `total_opex_costs`, but no separate top-level `total_opex`: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:369`, `allocate_opex.py:757`. That grid value is not reused. Missing total triggers research in `agentic_fit_allocator.py:115`, potentially again in `allocate_opex.py:811`. Failed research returns `total_opex_costs: None`; allocation then raises: `opex_research.py:792`, `allocate_opex.py:744`. Combined with finding 1, this explains permanent loading.

3. **Plausible long-running cause: sequential LLM calls and blocking citation checks.** L2 processes each L1 sequentially, allows three allocation attempts per parent, and configures SDK retries: `allocate_opex.py:280`, `allocate_opex.py:288`, `allocate_opex.py:315`. No application-level deadline wraps `ainvoke`: `allocate_opex.py:350`. Each allocation’s sanitizer can synchronously perform `requests.head(..., timeout=5, allow_redirects=True)` for every public source token, blocking that job’s event loop and heartbeat: `allocate_opex.py:455`, `capability_maps_generator.py:147`. This is a latency risk, not proof of an infinite hang.

4. **Confirmed recovery gap: Started/heartbeat calls lack `processKey`.** The monitor receives input/substep identifiers but no process identifier: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:355`. Durable Redis state requires all three; otherwise its key builder returns `None`: `genai_job_state.py:77`. Confirmed by an isolated check. Thus live pub/sub may work while durable heartbeat state is absent. Completion supplies `processKey`, but only after success. Recovery currently supports Capability Maps, not Impact Analyzer: `genai_job_recovery.py:284`.

5. **Conditional input-contract defects.** Conversion always expects a **flat, full L1/L2/L3 map**, even for L2-only work: `allocate_opex.py:839`, `allocate_opex.py:893`. Isolated checks reproduced `TypeError` for a hierarchical grid map and `KeyError: 'l3CapabilityName'` for an L2-only flat map. These exceptions reach finding 1. Separately, L2 requires L1 `capability_description`; missing it is swallowed into `state["error"]`: `allocate_opex.py:290`, `allocate_opex.py:461`. The caller never checks that error, so it can persist empty L2 output and report success rather than failure.

6. **Lower likelihood: project-name aliases can leave company name `None`.** Normalization copies aliases only when the destination key is absent, not null: `project_compat.py:59`. Verified: `projectName="Acme"` works; adding `clientName=None` leaves it `None`; uppercase `ProjectName` does not populate lowercase `clientName`. The endpoint reads only lowercase `projectInfo.clientName`: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:210`. A null company name did **not** break mocked L2 allocation; it mainly weakens any fallback research. I found no unconditional `None.lower()` failure on this path.

**Task Flow**
`POST /api/v1/agentic_fit_allocator` → HTTP 202 → synchronous BackgroundTasks runner → `asyncio.run(agentic_fit_allocation(...))`: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:156`, `base.py:24`.

- `"IA L2 Capabilities Opex"` and `"IA L1l2 Capabilities Opex"` select `GENERATE_L2_OPEX`: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:40`.
- Load persisted input → normalize project aliases → construct allocator → DB in-progress **25% twice** → publish Started: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:200`, `agentic_fit_allocator.py:238`.
- Load survey/files/workspace context → start monitor → allocate `["L2"]`: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:260`, `agentic_fit_allocator.py:347`.
- Monitor schedules an initial event and nominal 10-second heartbeats. `publish_message` attempts durable state, then Redis pub/sub; missing channel and Redis failures are logged without failing the job: `redis_cache.py:237`, `redis_cache.py:100`.
- Graph runs L1 → L2 → L3; L1/L3 skip for `["L2"]`. Success adds IDs → stops monitor → writes **75%** → adds descriptions → stores blob/output row → publishes Inserting/Generated → writes **100%** → publishes Completed → email: `allocate_opex.py:710`, `agentic_fit_allocator.py:356`, `agentic_fit_allocator.py:574`.
- **No backend assignment to 5% was found.** Progress events omit percentages until completion. The displayed 5% is therefore not traceable to an assignment in this repository; UI/orchestrator initialization remains unverified.
- `mark_completed`/`mark_failed` exist, but this endpoint calls neither; it maintains its own defective lifecycle: `genai_job_lifecycle.py:180`.

**L1 Contract**
L2 consumes supplied `capabilityInfo.AgenticFitGrid.l1_allocations`; it does **not** retrieve the previous L1 output using `OutputKey`: `allocate_opex.py:829`. Required fields are `capability_name`, `capability_description`, and numeric `amount_dollars`. Missing allocations default to `[]` and silently produce no L2 rows.

The October rename changed L1/L2 output producers consistently to `source_reasoning`. L2 does not read L1 reasoning at all. Mocked execution succeeded with either L1 key, and both L2 prompt templates formatted successfully. Residual prose mentions of `reasoning` can affect output quality, but no blocking key mismatch was found.

**Exception Handling**
Beyond the broken guard, `stop_progress()` awaits its task before any failure write; a task exception can escape the handler: `redis_cache.py:374`. Early initialization errors can also make the handler’s email arguments reference undefined `action`, `background_processing_data`, or `client_info`: `agentic_fit_allocator.py:625`. Email’s internal exceptions are swallowed, and normal completion is published before email, so email failure alone does not explain stuck L2: `email_send.py:234`. Telemetry backend exceptions are caught, although synchronous telemetry requests can add latency: `telemetry.py:150`.

**Workspace and Helpers**

`workspaceName` is available but optional in request schemas: `capability_maps_generator.py:40`, `missing_client_capabilities.py:51`, and the current/future process-flow schemas as `workspace_name` aliased to `workspaceName` (`current_process_flow_generator.py:24`, `future_process_flow_generator.py:26`). Its presence in a schema does not guarantee it exists in every export payload.

The existing `project_compat.py:113` `get_project_name()` helper resolves `projectName` first, with `clientName` and other casing variants as fallbacks. `project_compat.py:119` resolves workspace-name variants, and `get_project_info()` handles `projectInfo`/`clientInfo` nesting. These are suitable for the exports; direct `clientInfo.clientName` reads bypass that compatibility behavior.

**Minimal Proposal**

At each of the four read sites, use the existing getters and join the non-empty values. Preserve each site’s current fallback when both are absent:

```python
project_name = get_project_name(json_obj)
workspace_name = get_workspace_name(json_obj)
display_name = " - ".join(name for name in (project_name, workspace_name) if name)
```

Use `display_name` for the header/pool value; retain `""`, `"<< Add Pool Name >>"`, or `"Organization"` as that site’s fallback if `display_name` is empty. This is a proposal only; nothing was changed.
